ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e6475	Mr President. Dear Members of the Federal Government! Dear Mr. Vice-Chancellor – present here today in the representative of the Federal Chancellor! Ladies and gentlemen! Love all those who watch us today! For the current hour, we have today chosen the title “Why do you risk a generation,Corona”, Mr. Federal Chancellor?” – and yes, so we look not only at the present situation, but very deeply into the challenges, into the tasks in 2021, because one thing is very important to us: Especially in the event of such a crisis, it does not help to address the current challenges only from day to day, in view, but it is important to look at people with a perspective into the future and look forward to what challenges, what massive challenges we face our country, our society. Why are we doing that? – Well, Austria managed the second corona wave very badly in autumn in international comparison. The number of dead has now reached a really sad record level, and it continues to rise – regrettably – when you look at the number of people infected in higher age groups when you look at the number of people infected in old and nursing homes. So this government has not been able to protect those people who are exposed to the greatest risk of serious illness, the greatest risk of hospitalization, as that is, treatment in the hospital, but above all intensive medical care, and also the greatest risk of fatality in old and nursing homes. At the same time, the economy in Austria is breaking up in a dramatic way. The OECD sees Austria, considering the year 2020 and the forecasts for 2021 and 2022, in second place behind Portugal in terms of recovery. So we are really bad there – in terms of health numbers, in terms of death rates, but also in terms of economy and unemployment. There is an increasing number of unemployed people; and new jobs, new jobs, upswing, confidence that all is not in sight with your policies. I know I've heard this many times now. For the government and the government parties, the other ones are always to blame. Hell, these are the others, we know that from literature. These are the people in Austria who are at risk, the citizens, that is the opposition, these are the federal states, these are the boys who have made some sort of disproportionate party. Let us now look together into the future! In 2021, we will hopefully have overcome the health aspect of the crisis, thanks to a vaccination that will be widely available and that will also really work and provide protection. Other remains a society that may even know single winners: Many people who are not affected by a secure job in the state environment, i.e. federal staff, national staff, but also in parts of the private sector, and also people who are in retirement, will have no income loss and no dramatic loss of opportunity. But it will also be a society that will know massive losers: The unemployed, the self-employed, and many, many young people. Once more – you already know this from us NEOS, we say it since March – we urge a balance in the fight against crisis, even for 2021, when we are on our way out of the crisis. Health, society, education, social affairs and the economy – all this must be in the perspective of political decisions. But we also demand a balance with regard to those who are particularly hard hit by the crisis. These are currently old people in nursing and old homes who do not protect you enough. These are young people in the future who will rob you of opportunities, prospects and futures. The crisis hits the young generation twice: at present and directly as regards their lives as regards their education as regards access to a teaching centre, which may be the chance of getting a first job in working life. She meets the students who are just beginning the first semester and in distance school. It meets young people who have no social contacts at a time when this is so important for personal development. It meets the young people who had planned a semester abroad and could not do so. It meets the young people who are among the losers of education who could not be reached in the time of home schooling. It is precisely the ones who already have massive difficulties in starting up and which policy should actually ensure equal opportunities. It meets young self-employed people who do not receive orders; this is a dramatic situation at the very beginning of self-employment, when you approach the matter with hope and confidence. She meets young people who are already unemployed because a new job is currently de facto impossible. We must therefore act urgently in the interests of our children, in the interests of the young – and we must really be active! – to address reforms, to secure the labour market for young people, to secure teaching facilities, to safeguard educational opportunities, and, above all, to address the social impact of the Corona crisis on these young people. And yes, ladies and gentlemen, this too is a marathon and not a sprint – like the Corona fight – with a press conference and a headline you do not get through. But the good thing is: Proposals are on the table. Proposals from us, including from other political groups, are on the table, and what we are now expecting is a plan and a strategy, which is a strategy that does not lead to the fact that we once have to say that we have a lost generation, a generation of Corona. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, the alarm bells are ringing – what you see when you look at education, how many children and young people have not been reached there, how chances have been robbed and what this can mean for their further life course; when you look at labour market data: 38 000 young people are without work, tending to rise, a 26 percent increase compared to the previous year, and 8 000 more young people. Or: Teachers' market. As of September, the number of open apprenticeships fell by 19.4 percent compared to the previous year. There are 7 600 teaching places on 10 500 job seekers. How to say so beautifully: That's not going to work out. This has an impact on the employment biography, which is dramatic and long-term. In addition, your policy of the cost-es-what-es-will of the debt backpack is getting bigger and bigger. That means that our children will have to bathe out at one time. On the other hand, when it comes to ensuring sustainable social networks for these boys, we must admit that we are already spending EUR 24 billion on pensions this year, because our contribution system is not enough to ensure this for our young people. We have to fight against this urgently. This crisis has shown where we need reform everywhere. Turn on little screws and give press conferences that cost a lot of PR money – a lot of PR money you can get back to! – that may have been so far. That's enough in good weather times, but not in times of crisis. We must now take seriously the reforms for young people, for the good future of our country, and for these reforms we need a broad basis. I therefore call for a future Convention, based on the Constitutional Convention, which unfortunately never really came into force in many areas. Let's go – from Parliament – a future convention! Let us devote ourselves to the questions of the opportunities of the future, of innovation, of the prosperity of the future, but of course also to the questions of federalism, because we simply cannot afford to reject ourselves, to present press conferences and headlines and to rob the young people of the future. [[Congratulations with the NEOS.]] So we're shredding the sleeves – that's my proposal – we're picking up for 2021, we're putting the year 2021 under the motto: Confidence and future. The crisis and the opportunities of young people are at the heart of the crisis – this crisis is enough to finally do so – so that we can confidently go forward into an innovative future that offers all people opportunities for prosperity. - Thank you many times.

[[towards FPÖ]] Women's disdainful, misogyne old men's sycamores, sexist advertising, verbal insults, obscene-vulgary obscurity on the street: Every woman sitting here today, every woman who is listening to me right now, I would almost say that every woman in all of Europe knows exactly what I am talking about. Cigarettes that are dampened on naked skin, a broken nose, swollen eyes, bloody side wounds in the face, painful hematomas all over the body, bruises and bone fractures, intimate touch against one's own will when a penis or something else is penetrated into one's own body against one's own will: Statistically speaking, every third woman across the EU knows what I am talking about. ==References====External links==* Official website Year of life is affected by physical and/or sexual violence. This shocking violence is evidence of a survey of violence against women carried out by the European Union Agency for Fundamental Rights in 2014. The figures are shocking, but they are, above all, an urgent act, an action order to us all, to the policy to implement measures against violence against women and girls across Europe with the necessary determination – measures to which Austria has also committed itself with the ratification of the so-called Istanbul Convention. Istanbul Convention is the most important legal instrument against violence against women and provides for very comprehensive measures to combat this violence to raise awareness, to prevent violence, to protect victims and to effectively prosecute the perpetrators. An independent committee of experts has evaluated the implementation status of the Istanbul Convention in Austria in 2014 and has attested us in some areas very urgent follow-up needs. The Federal Government has implemented important improvements in many, then entirely criticised by the experts over the course of the year, four of which I would like to highlight. The first point concerns hatred on the network. In principle, hatred in the web can meet each and every one of us. From studies we know that hatred is increasing in the web against women, against people from the LGTIQ community and against people with migration biography – most often also decided against Muslims. Thus, it is directed against those people who are often marginalized and structurally disadvantaged several times. The result is that these people withdraw from the Internet. With a Europe-wide bill against hatred on the network, which we hope to adopt with broad approval later, we will now create the framework conditions with which those affected can easily, quickly and cost-effectively oppose hatred on the network in the future. We are thus setting up a central measure to protect against violence in the digital sector as provided for in the Istanbul Convention. World around, and that's good and important. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The second point is the legal and psychosocial follow-up to the Istanbul Convention for children and young people who become witnesses to violence. Why is that important? – Because children often live in inner family violent relationships for years. They hear the screams from the side room, they see the mother or siblings being beaten, and they often suffer trauma that can have lifelong consequences. So far, there has been a huge gap which the Minister of Justice is now closing. From 2021, these children, the mute witnesses of violence, will be granted the status of victims. In future, they will also receive legal process support and psychosocial support. Violence experts speak of a milestone in the protection of children and young people. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] The third point I would like to highlight is the increase in the means of protection of violence and the protection of victims. We have significantly increased the funds for the protection of violence and the protection of victims: in justice, in the ministry of women, in the Ministry of the Interior and in other ministries. The Minister of Justice has achieved an important trend in the judicial budget, enabling important improvements in victim protection to be implemented. This was very, very urgent and very, very important. The Federal Government has also increased by 43 percent the budget of the Ministry of Women, from which very many anti-poverty and anti-violence measures are financed centrally. Ten years of red-black governments were equivalent to stagnant means, the turquoise-blue government has finally cut the funds in the women's budget, so finally there is the urgently necessary significant increase in resources. I always say it and say it here: It is also clear that further increases must follow. Further increases will follow so that we can further strengthen the network of violence in Austria and expand it more closely. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I come to the fourth and last point, namely the victim's defence-oriented work. Why is this important? – Because we can only break the spiral of violence if we work with those who practice violence, and these are the perpetrators. This is not deliberately opposed because it is actually mainly men who exercise violence. At the last meeting of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, we decided on a five-party proposal, and I would like to express my express thanks to all the political groups. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] These four examples I have selected show that Austria is working decisively and consistently on the implementation of the Istanbul Convention. At the same time – we are at the hour of Europe – it is, however, challenged and eroded in other European countries. Slovakia, Bulgaria, Hungary and Poland want to get out of the European Convention against Violence against Women. In the latter two countries, women and also LGTIQ rights have long been under permanent fire. LGBTIQ-free zones were recently established in Poland, and now the legal-conservative government wants to strengthen its violence by cutting the reproductive self-determination rights of women and imposing a ban on abortion. ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e9036	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! Today's European hour, entitled 'Europe-wide violence against women', takes up a very important issue, because violence is a general social issue that we can only solve together. Unfortunately, violence against women in Austria and Europe does not belong to the past. If we look at the figures on the femicides, for example, the women's murders, we also see shocking figures in Austria. In 2019, 39 women were murdered in Austria, many of them from their ex-partners or family members. 2018 represents a sad record year: This year, 41 women were murdered. However, it is not necessary to start murders at all to find out how omnipresent violence against women is, not only in Austria, but throughout Europe. Every third woman is affected by violence worldwide. In the EU, half of women experience sexual harassment or assaults from the age of 15. Year of life. These figures, ladies and gentlemen, are shocking, because each one of us knows according to these statistics a woman who has experienced violence, each and every one of us knows a woman who has experienced sexual harassment. This means that violence against women is real, violence against women is everywhere, and it is unfortunately sad everyday life. At European level, we have put in place numerous measures to combat violence against women in Europe. I am in regular exchange with the Ministers of Justice and Justice of other Member States. There are a number of measures that have been launched and which are currently being discussed. Finally, there were measures against violence and hatred on the network, which were also discussed as a topic at the EU's informal Council of Justice Ministers. The work programme of the Portuguese Presidency has also been presented, and one of the priorities is the EU strategy for victim rights, which is to be given a high priority under the Portuguese Presidency. At the same time, the protection of the vulnerable persons should also be of particular importance. There will already be a conference on this subject in the first half of the year, where it is hoped that the first measures will be launched. Moreover, this has already been mentioned, it will also be discussed whether the EU as a whole can join the Istanbul Convention. The Council of the European Union is eagerly waiting for the Court's opinion on whether accession would be permitted. In my view, accession would, in any case, be important and correct. All Member States have signed the Convention, 21 have ratified it, and the Council will continue to influence the fact that the Istanbul Convention is also ratified by the other Member States. We therefore hope that there will be a lot of movement and many measures at European level that we can jointly set in motion. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] In the Ministry of Justice, we have also put in place a number of measures at national level to combat violence against women. At the beginning of the corona pandemic, we deliberately accepted this problem, because the Minister of Women and I have assembled and seen ourselves: All over the world, where a lockdown was imposed, there are problems with violence against women. Especially the situation of an enhanced lockdown, in which one has to spend time together around the clock in a household, in a tight space, can lead to an escalation of violence against women and the family. We have therefore put in place numerous measures. I was in contact with the intervention agency at this time, and also later with numerous women's organisations, with non-violent organisations, because it was simply important to learn from the experiences of the lockdown and from the measures we have put in place. We also had a non-violent meeting with the Alliance yesterday, where we exchanged experiences with the Lockdown in particular. We have also recognised that we are able to bring numerous measures that we put in place at the time into permanent law. To this end, I would like to give a few examples: We have seen in the lockdown that it would be important to be able to apply for a interim order by electronic means to court. The many, many positive feedback we have received has moved us to bring this measure into the permanent right. Why is that so important? Why is it important for women to be able to apply for interlocutory measures to protect against violence by electronic means? – Mein Ladies and gentlemen, many women don't trust themselves in the house, many women are afraid to be seen on their way to court, they are afraid that their violent husband might know that she's just going to court. Therefore, there is now the possibility that the victim protection organisations can represent the woman concerned and, on behalf of the woman, apply for a preliminary order in the field of violence. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I believe that this is a very great relief for many, many women, not only in the lockdown, but in general. As mentioned by Mr Disoski, we have also greatly expanded the area of victim protection. We have increased the victim protection budget by 4.2 million euros in total, and we have expanded this victim protection to include hatred and violence on the Internet. This is so important because hatred and violence, violence against women – this structural violence against women – often begin on the Internet. It is therefore important to give women support at these moments, not only psychosocial support, but also legal process support, so that they can be effective, effective, quick and, above all, courageous. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] We've also expanded the protection of victims for children. Children are the silent witnesses of violence, especially domestic violence, and children are very often re-traumatized when they have to testify in court. So far, many children who have been traumatised have depended on donations, or the women's protection organisations, which had to rely on donations to support these children. We now have the process support for Children experiencing domestic violence, who experience how their mothers are beaten, widened so that they too receive psychosocial and legal process support. [[Congratulation with Greens and ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, the fight against violence against women is a social task. This fight not only affects justice, it not only affects the police, it not only affects the women's resort, it affects us all. If we want to change something if we as the Federal Government want to change something if we want to change something as a society if we want to reduce violence against women massively, we also need to talk about power relations. We need to talk about sexism, we need to talk about the dangerous consequences of patriarchal structures. We must also address the overall social causes of violence against women. There is an SOS function in the public security app in Spain; it is uncomplicated: wipe once, press once, and you call the police directly. Mrs Minister, that would be something for us too, Copy-and-paste would certainly be allowed. [[towards Federal Minister Raab]] There were 64 meetings on the Beijing Action Programme, the termination of practices such as female genital mutilation and child marriage, early marriage, forced marriage – all these are United Nations objectives. There are rising serious problems. What does the EU do? – It gives political declaration of will, unfortunately without legal force. There is the EU strategy for gender equality, Istanbul Convention, the Convention on Violence and Harassment in the Work Environment and, since 2019, the call for a strategy to prevent and combat violence. In 2014, there was a resolution to add violence against women of serious crime. There are demands and resolutions, but no reduction in the number of crimes, no fall in the number of violence against women. In some countries the rate of prosecution is low, the conviction of domestic violence is difficult, the judicial systems are different. What does Austria do? – The regulation of violence prevention advice will only affect the first violent perpetrators in the middle of next year. Parliament is dealing with the development of victim protection and representation in the case of interlocutory orders. Is that all right? But it is obvious that one of the women's ministers finally recognized: Violence has tradition in certain communities. There is a new brochure focusing on violence in the context of culture and tradition. I can quote from this: “The family or community does not see a problem in ‘violence in the name of honor’, but in the daughters who do not want to bow to traditional forms of violence. [...] The daughter [...] is the enemy.’ ‘A successful fight against ‘honcultural’ or traditional forms of violence requires the involvement of men.’ My respect for this knowledge, but you could have had that before! We have always warned of this, we have put forward proposals, but these have not found a majority. Violence against women concerns us all, politics and society. The EU will not solve the problem. There are two ministers here now, but each may feel addressed: Mrs Minister, strengthen your commitment and influence! Store You women from violence by repeaters who got away last time! It needs delivery standards for the victims, claim to timely therapy. Strengthen the awareness-raising programs of the security authorities and the forensic trace protection in the event of violence against women! And: It is also necessary to monitor bans on entry. Dear Mrs Minister, ensure a corresponding interface between domestic violence and child protection, stimulate self-defense courses in schools and, Minister of Women, finally close the gaps in care! In Austria, there are still 100 women's homes missing.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e9989	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear women ministers! No feminism without anti-racism and no anti-racism without feminism: Almost 30 years ago, 24 women from all over the world, female feminists, political activists from countries of the global north and the global south, were together at Rutgers University campus in New Jersey. They came and invented the 16 days against violence against women. Of course, this was not simply an invention, the action was based on years and decades of feminist struggles for women's rights, against violence against women, for the self-determination of women and against repression. Today, the 16 days are one of the most recognised and longest campaigns for women's rights worldwide. It began at a time before the Internet, was thought of as a bridge between women's rights and human rights, even before we spoke of women's rights as human rights, and over the years has been made a global movement by thousands of feminist activists and feminist organizations. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Fire brigades.]] The 16 days were always holistic, always inclusive and always intersectional. Self-determination, independence and safety: These are protection factors for women against violence – not prohibitions, not clothing regulations, non-disciplinary, non-repressions, non-deceptions, non-criminals, neither in private nor in public areas. Fighting violence against women must mean violence against all women, from every side and in every form. We cannot talk about women's rights without talking about all women. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] And we cannot talk about women's rights without talking today, in particular, about the rights of Muslims and their concern about anti-Muslim racism, a discriminatory and violent experience and for a lot of life's reality. Victims of anti-Muslim violence are mostly visible Muslims who wear headscarves. They are at the centre of media debates in which they are spoken about either as victims of oppression or as perpetrators of so-called political Islam. They are the objective of policy measures, which are supposed to want their emancipation. We cannot talk about women's rights without talking about all women. Zara's advisory body reports that a direct relationship to the attacks is often made with the accusation of Muslims as terrorists. The reported attacks are only the tip of the iceberg. Recently, the Court of Justice has presented its report to the private sector, and it notes that discrimination and anti-Muslim racism in the working world are primarily directed against Muslims wearing a headline. The people concerned often do not know that they can defend themselves and where they can report discrimination. Often they are too intimidated, they think it's better to keep silent than to make themselves more vulnerable. There, volunteers do tireless work by documenting and publishing incidents, supporting those affected, holding information events and focusing on empowering those concerned. I would like to thank the whole team very much and the most cordial congratulations and a lot of strength for this valuable and important work. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Fighting violence against women must mean violence against all women, from every side and in every form. The 16 days against violence against women are popular, and this is Well so, no question, but they're not an accessory and not a fig leaf. They cannot be instrumentalized in the fight against women – not against sex workers, not migrants, not Muslims, not against the right to abortion, not against sexual rights, not against women's rights. Finally, without feminism, no anti-racism and no anti-racism, no feminism! - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e10347	Madam President. Dear ministers! Ladies and gentlemen! It is a matter of course, of course: Each country fights violence, including violence against women and children. There is, above all, criminal law to do that, as appropriate means must be provided to ensure effective prosecution, and of course one must already invest in prevention. The Istanbul Convention is a legally binding instrument and requires States to act effectively against violence against women. However, several previous speakers have already mentioned morally again that there are countries that do not implement the Convention they reject – and that is why my previous speakers put it to the speaker. My previous speaker, too, advocates Hungary and Poland: Because they did not implement the Convention, did not ratify it, they would incite or condone violence on women, and would not speak out against violence against women. That's not right! It must also be addressed why these countries are opposed to the implementation of the Istanbul Convention and that it is not exclusively devoted to the fight against real violence against women, but that, even if it has already been disputed today, there is an ideology behind it that can be rejected. Hungary and Poland are very likely to have sufficient criminal law to combat violence against women, and they also have women's homes and prevention measures. However, they oppose the ideology that is to be imposed on them – and in my opinion it is right. In fact, the Convention assumes that the cause of violence is always in education and is predetermined in childhood. That's why young boys now have to learn that girls and boys are equivalent. – Yes, of course, but there should be sex education for young children again, one must work on it and raise it; but not the families should raise the children, but the state should intervene. It includes the approach that the sexes are not biological, but social constructions; and it is stated that this sex is not determined by biology and nature, but by social roles and behaviours that are assigned to men and women. But not everyone sees it like that. The Convention simply ignores the natural differences between men and women, and differences in behaviour are only social and are already discriminatory. So you have to be very careful when it comes to violence. We understand among them real physical violence. In this convention, however, the concept of violence is defined very, very far and excessive, because, for example, a family life with a classical division of roles – if the woman is more dedicated to child rearing and more domestic work than the man – is already considered to be, as it were, an outdated pattern of behaviour that was socially assigned and is already regarded as part of structural violence. But I firmly reject that. This is the way Hungary and Poland do, and they have every right to do so without being shocked that they did nothing against violence against women. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] I can only say to the Corona policy: It will already be so that violence against women and also against children or the conflicts and aggressions have increased. But above all, the government parties and the parties that supported the lockdown must be taken into account. It's better to start by looking at lockdowns, school closures and so on as short as possible or not repeated. [[Congratulations on the members of the FPÖ.]] It is also a mistake in this Convention that it is a significant concern only for relation states, which have to be fought and punished, but it does not deal with the attacks on the streets, but which are also a major problem – and I wonder if we are not ahead of Hungary and Poland. I therefore think that it is totally inappropriate to go about these countries; the same applies to the rule of law debate: Only ideology is to be imposed. Thank you.

The area of so-called upskirting – I remember my request, Mrs Minister, which I introduced in December 2019 – has also been taken into account. The unwanted photography especially under the skirt of women or the making of nude photos or nude videos without the knowledge or consent of those affected are now expressly punished. The SPÖ will therefore give its approval to this package of laws in the judicial part, because it will take a step in the right direction. However, a wormhead drop is that the penalty framework for simply making these images has been reduced from the originally planned one year to six months. Thus, the threat of punishment in international comparison is very low. To name it clearly: It's about sexual violence against women, it's about crossing borders, it's about improper exercise of power. To take home intimate photos and then possibly spread in the web is a serious attack on the integrity of people, especially of women and girls. How did that look in the past? – In the past it was so that many affected people, many women, many girls simply didn’t dare to go this way because the way to court, to judgment was a long one, lasted forever, took a lot of money – sometimes that went to the four-digit area – and much of that, as one had to find out at the end of the day, wasn’t even complaining. This has led to a lot of costs and, finally, to the fact that many people have not taken up the task. But what were the consequences? – The consequences were that people who wanted to express themselves on the Internet have withdrawn many women, many girls, from the Internet, because they just didn’t want to give themselves this shame, because they just didn’t want to put up with this hatred and hate, and they have left the Internet to a small group of loud and aggressive people. In doing so, this small group of lutes and aggressive people destroyed our conversation culture, our respectful dealings with each other. This law is also so important because it returns: It makes the Internet, this space, again a space where people want to express themselves, where people also have the opportunity to express their thoughts, without any danger of being exposed to hatred or hatred. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Leave You may give me a brief overview of the most important improvements we propose today! Much of it has already been mentioned, so I would pick out just a few highlights: First of all – and I am really glad that we did –: We have launched a new procedure. We have allowed for a new haste procedure which will also allow those affected by human dignity to move quickly and cost-effectively to the labour market. With an online form to be sent to a district court, one can request that the statement, this posting is quickly deleted and thus no longer spread on the Internet. Secondly, When the threshold of criminal law is reached: We have also made some improvements there in order to actually address today's problems of communication in the 21st century. ==References== We have intensified in criminal law in the field of cybermobbing. So far, the cybermobbing crime has only been fulfilled if it was a recurrence case. In the future, it will be possible that this will be punishable as cybermobbing even in the event of a one-time offense. We have also intensified the situation in the case of injurement. So far, it has been so that, after the crime, one was punished only if the insult referred to a whole group. Now it is also possible to be punished when the insult is pronounced against a person, and only because it belongs to a certain group. The upsizing ban has already been mentioned. Unfortunately, it is a phenomenon that is becoming more and more widespread in society, especially in the youthful scene, where one simply photographs unseen under the skirt, in the shame area. That is what we have now put under punishment. One of the most important points I would like to stress here is the protection of victims. It was especially important to me personally that the protection of victims be extended and that it be extended to the crime of violence and hatred on the Internet – that is because many women and many young women suffer enormously when they are insulted on the Internet. Many don't trust to go to school because they've been exposed to this hatred and this shit. It is therefore important to put this tool in their hands so that they can be really brave against insults and against these hatreds and hatreds. That is why we have expanded victim protection and made it possible to provide psychosocial and legal support for typical hate-in-net offences. What is the cost of this right of measurement now? – It is true, as my previous speaker said, that the ECJ is going to explain this very far and this directive speaks of total costs, but I believe that these considerations, which lead to a narrowing of this interpretation, are quite appropriate. What is the closer understanding of these costs? – It is clear that those costs that are announced to the consumer when entering its credit relationship and which concern the overall burden of the credit are included, and that, however, those costs, such as notarial costs or intermediation costs for the credit, are no longer included. In contrast to my previous speakers, I also believe that it is appropriate if the entry into force of this law takes place at the time of the judgment, but if the effect is only to be achieved for those loans that are repaid after 1.1.2021, because – and this is a fundamental issue of the legal system – one should be very careful with retroactive effects, because it is ultimately also about the protection of trust. That was what led us to do, and not the fact that we want to see someone or the banking sector do something about it. It should also be noted that there should be a fair application of these provisions in the mortgage and real estate credit law. All in all, this is a welcome change in favour of consumers, who are quite appropriate and who are in line with the interests of all those involved, especially consumers.

That is what we now allow with this law in the extension and where we have to think on some points about whether or not we are somehow putting it into the permanent right – which we do very well with some of this package of laws. We allow associations to hold their urgently needed annual general meetings – and that was a big question at the beginning of this year – digitally. We allow meetings in company law, which can be held digitally, up to and including – we change the legal profession right – to the general meetings of the individual Bars, which can also be held in this way. A particularly important point, from my point of view, is that we can continue to hold the hearings in courts on a digital basis – this is a very crucial point, because we often drive 30, 40, 50 kilometres to a district court for a 15-minute date, in which only one third is agreed between two lawyers and the judge, how the procedure runs, what evidence is recorded and when it takes place. It is a particularly positive point of this innovation that this will continue to be possible. I do not hide that I share the scepticism of those who believe that evidence is not necessarily appropriate for this. In my opinion, the immediate impression of a witness or document to be heard will prevent this from happening. But I can already comfort those who report scepticism with the indication that the judges in this country know very well what is possible, and in practice implement it exactly as well and ask before whether it can be discharged digitally. Therefore: In practice no problem! It does not take place if the parties do not agree. I would also like to thank you, Mrs Minister, and the main committee, for having managed to pay the flat rate for the many procedural aids – 20 000 cases a year – which the lawyers provide for the people free of charge, this flat rate fee used for pensions, at last on the rate of 50 per cent, so that they will receive 21 million euros as a bill in the future. However, there is probably a possibility to access the deposit, when tenants and landlords agree that the rest or the rental delay will be paid out of the deposit and therefore not automatically a filling obligation will be created. So it is very likely to access the deposit. As I said, however, this requires an agreement and must not cause filling. There are two regulations that are provided for in this package, which we are putting into the permanent right, because they have proved themselves in practice. On the one hand, this is the possibility to establish the electronic notarial acts and to carry out certain notarial acts by electronic means. Furthermore, we have extended the possibility of simplified GmbH-foundation via the company service portal because we have seen that it has proven that simplified GmbH-foundations can now also be carried out electronically. ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e16960	Mr President. Mrs Bundesministerins! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Citizens' values! With the two government documents we present today, we valorize the annual reparations to the Catholic, the evangelical and the Old Catholic Church as well as to the Israeli religious society. ==References== It was decided on July 1st, that today we should put it in legal form. It is not a promotion, it is a compensation for goods seized by the Nazi regime and no longer restored by the Republic. The basis of the payments to the Catholic Church is the 1960 asset agreement between Austria and the Holy See. After the end of 1938, the religious fund had been seized by the Nazi regime; priests – until 1939 – were then paid a modest salary and the church building burden was borne. Austria has already committed itself to restitution payments with the Vienna State Treaty in 1955. These restitution payments are made up of two parts according to the asset agreement: a variable, which is evaluated annually, and a fixed amount, which is then valorised only when inflation exceeds the 20 percent mark. This happened in 2018, and that is why we will make this adjustment retroactively from 2018 onwards. The annual fixed amount is increased by 3.4 million euros and now totals 20.7 million euros. At the same time, we have adapted the payments to the Protestant Church, to the Old Catholic Church and to the Israeli religious society accordingly. And so that we can make it a little easier in the future in the adaptation process, if this threshold is exceeded by 20 percent, the valorisation by regulation or by exchange of notes. Ladies and gentlemen! I think that all of this makes sense, is right and necessary, and it is especially important that it is fixed in a contractual way. The agreement is a sign of the good cooperation of Austria with the various churches and religious communities. I am convinced that this is the right way, and the unanimity in the Constitutional Committee has confirmed this. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e17368	Mr President. Mr. Finanzminister, who was just here! High house! Estimated Austrians! I am speaking to TOP 15, to amend the Account Registry and Account Statement Act. The revision of § 4 para. 5 Accounting and Accounting Act is referred to by Minister of Finance Blümel in the explanatory notes as ‘language adjustment’, which serves ‘the clarification’. In reality, this is not a clarification, but an attack on the rule of law. The proposed new regulation would mean that, in the course of an audit or external audit, access to the account register can be carried out without any reason, even if it is merely a routine periodic audit and there is no reasonable and substantial suspicion of the inaccuracy of the declaration of deposit. In this context, I should like to quote from the opinion of the board of tax advisors and auditors of 20.7.2020: “The audit to start in reverse with an account registry insight, on which an information in accordance with § 4 para. 6 KontRegG, without regard to any personal and professional sphere that is also protected by constitutional law.” – Meetingperson could not be formulated. [[I should like to congratulate the FPÖ and the representatives of the NEOS.]] In the course of an audit, the taxable person should also be given the opportunity to disperse any doubts as to the accuracy of the declaration by means of a corresponding clarification before a review into the account register takes place. [[Abg. Loacker: If the police are allowed to go to the apartment, the... can look into the account!]] With regard to the proposed amendment, it should also be noted that it is not compatible with the constitutional rule on banking secrecy, § 38 Banking Act, or that it requires a constitutional majority. According to § 38 para. 2 Banking Act, which constitutes a constitutional provision, will limit banking secrecy only in terms of information according to § 4 Account Register Act – that is the regulation that existed at the time of the regulation of § 38 BWG. This is a static, not a dynamic reference. The further restriction of banking secrecy therefore requires a constitutional provision. This legal view is also represented by the Austrian Bar Association. For the same reasons, the proposed amendment to § 8 para. 3 To refuse to take account of accounts and accounts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e17430	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! I refer to agenda item 16: Federal Law on the New Credit Agreements with the International Monetary Fund. It is essentially about empowering the Austrian National Bank to grant the International Monetary Fund, on behalf of the Republic and under these New Credit Agreements, a credit framework of up to EUR 3 636 980 000 in special drawing rights. This is hardly a change for Austria, because the National Bank would have been able to make 3,6 billion euros available under the legislation in force. Overall, however, it is important because enforcement in all Member States will lead to a doubling of these special drawing rights. This will create more opportunities for the International Monetary Fund to help where necessary. We know that the International Monetary Fund is a central pillar of the international financial structure. This fund is financed by the quota, i.e. from the funds paid by each Member State. In Austria, as has been said, the Austrian National Bank is authorized to act for the Republic. As of April 2020, the IMF has 189 Member States. Among the larger are the USA, Japan, China, Germany, Great Britain, France and Italy. For example, Austria holds 0.83 percent capital of the votes. If a member has payment difficulties, it can claim assistance from the IMF. In addition, under certain conditions, temporary loans shall be granted, to be reimbursed at interest rates. We know examples in which this has worked very well in the recent past. Romania, Argentina, Greece or Ireland have benefited from such loans. In addition, the International Monetary Fund also supports developing countries in Africa, Asia or South America in the development of economic concepts and supports them through direct financial aid from the giving Member States. Similar to lending, development cooperation is usually linked to conditions such as democracy strengthening or corruption reduction. In times of crisis, the funds are often insufficient to provide effective assistance and to meet the needs. Then these new credit agreements will be activated. This is the case especially in times of the Covid pandemic. The New Credit Agreements include additional funds made available to the IMF by 40 States. I have already described the title of the draft law to be adopted today. There is currently a need for action because these new credit agreements would expire at the end of 2022 and are intended to increase them in total. If we increase from EUR 3.6 billion to EUR 3.637 billion, this means an increase of 1 percent for Austria. However, Austria is making an urgent contribution to globalisation, to global financial market stability and complying with its international obligations. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e19042	Madam President. Dear Members of the Federal Government! High house! Estimated Austrians! I speak to TOP 22, to the Federal Law, with the promotion of the federal government due to the Covid-19-Pandemy linked to the tax charity. The funding for the coronary aid measures is financed in full from the contributions of Austrian taxpayers, so it should also be a matter of course that tax sinners do not receive corona promotions or, if tax compensations have been received, they must also repay them. The federal law introduced by the Black and Green Government Coalition, which connects corona promotions to fiscal welfare, does not meet the legal objective – no promotions for tax offenders. With this Tax Health Protection Act – as I would like to call it – Black-Green ensures that taxpayers do not have to repay benefits received. I would now like to refer to the individual provisions of this federal law. § 1 of this Federal Act ensures that entrepreneurs can behave unfairly at the end of the grant and yet do not have to repay the grants. § 4 of this Federal Act ensures that tax sinners must pay back subsidies only if the funding agency becomes aware of the misused behaviour of the taxpayer within five years. Especially scandalous is § 9. § 9 of this Federal Act ensures that tax sinners do not have to repay the benefits received if the legal basis of the funding has already been adopted in 2020, but this will be the case for most corona aid measures. § 10 of this Federal Act ensures that tax sinners do not have to repay the benefits received if the abusive behaviour of the tax sinner has not been discovered until after 31.12.2025 or the subsidies have not been recovered until 31.12.2025. Let us now come to the black and green definition of fiscal welfare in § 3. According to § 3 Z 2, for example, there is fiscal welfare even if the company has paid a maximum of EUR 500 000 each year to Group companies in tax havens or to low-tax countries. However, if the trader forgets a disclosure in the corporate tax declaration, the trader shall nevertheless be considered taxable if the forgotten interest and licence fees do not exceed the amount of EUR 100 000. The explanations of this federal law speak generously of a tolerance limit. Therefore, certain amounts, interest rates and licence fees up to EUR 100 000 are not to be specified in the tax declaration. This falls below the border of the bagatelles, and this is still considered taxable. – Isn't that inconceivable? [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] For example, according to paragraph 4, entrepreneurs who commit only financial irregularities within the meaning of the Financial Penal Code are also considered taxable. I am now allowed to deal with these small tax sins, which can be committed according to the definition of black-green, but which are nevertheless regarded as tax-recognition. An illegality of financial regulation according to § 49 of the Financial Criminal Law is, for example, a guilty person who deliberately asserts unfair tax credits by surrendering unfair VAT pre-applications. That is to say, the intentional submission of an incorrect VAT pre-registration is no problem for Black-Green. For example, an illegality of financial regulation under § 49b of the Financial Criminal Law is guilty of the intention to infringe the obligation to submit the country-related report in accordance with the Accounting Price Documentation Act by the fact that reported items are not transmitted to the Financial Office or are improperly transmitted to the Financial Office. This is not a problem for black and green either. For example, an illegal financial regulation pursuant to § 49c of the Financial Criminal Law is also guilty of the intentional violation of a duty under the provisions of the EU Notification Act by the fact that incorrect information is reported. I may point out briefly that the EU Notification Act is about reporting cross-border tax designs to the Financial Office. If one neglects or violates this reporting obligation, that is no problem for Black-Green at all. I would like to save you more details of this Black-Green Tax Health Protection Act. Through this federal law, the black and green government coalition protects not taxpayers, but tax sinners. We will certainly not agree to this Tax Health Protection Act and accordingly have tabled an amendment which has already been distributed or is about to be distributed. I believe that I have given sufficient reason to this request, which essentially reaffirms the points I have put forward here. Thank you. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The previous speakers have partly addressed details of other agenda items, but I would really like to take a step back and ask the question in a little detail: What does an eco-social tax reform really need? – You might be able to intervene – Mr Krainer often argues – in order to intervene in a regulatory policy; one could, for example, prohibit buying larger cars, one could require that tickets for public transport be part of a salary or wage, one could require that goods be transported on the rail, one could also prescribe repairs of broken or broken items. In some areas such regulatory access may be useful and necessary, but the eco-social tax reform is much more elegant access. It creates the possibility that people still have the power to decide to choose one thing or another – if you absolutely want to, you can still buy the thickest instincts – and you just lose the advantage that you can hand over and transfer the costs that arise for the general public to the public. Instead, you have to pay for the costs yourself – that is the great advantage of the eco-social tax reform. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Eßl.]] In short: You are no longer the defyer, if you behave climate-friendlyly, but you have a financial advantage in your behavior. The second major advantage of the eco-social tax reform, of taxes as a means of combating climate change, is taxation. Taxes, and there are some curious examples in the history: I remember the window control in the 19th century. In the 19th century in the UK or in France, people have lost their windows in their homes, up to cheap rental houses that no longer had windows to save themselves money. By this you can see: Taxation and tax reform are effective and help reduce emissions if we use them properly, and I believe that we are doing so with these next steps in the course of the eco-social tax reform. This makes repairs more interesting and does not throw away everything, but gives the matter a second chance if that is possible. I believe that what has now happened is a first important step, it is not the entire tax reform. ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e19459	My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Since the aid has already been raised several times in the previous debate, perhaps a brief overview. We as the Federal Government have so far provided a very large volume of support for jobs and businesses in this difficult time, ranging from short-time work, guarantees, support to direct aid. So far, 28.5 billion euros have been legally committed or already flown. With the fixed-cost grant phase one and two as well as the turnover replacement, almost 140 000 applications with a total volume of 2.5 billion euros have been submitted by companies. Already 1.8 billion euros have been paid. An analysis of the International Monetary Fund, taken up in Austria by the IHS, has shown: Austria has the largest rescue package in relation to economic performance within the European Union, namely 8 percent of GDP in total, which is twice as much as the EU average of 4 percent of GDP. This can really be seen in support of jobs and businesses, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] You know, the lockdown has to be partially extended in order to continue to reduce the infection. That is why we have also extended revenue revenue to December. The companies that must continue to be closed will receive 50 percent of turnover in December by the end of the year. As has already been proven, the application is made via financial online. From 16 December, this December replacement will be applicable for the closed establishments. This will be an additional cost of about 1 billion euros. Of course, sales can't be a long-term measure, that's completely clear. As one of two countries within the European Union, we are therefore building a fixed cost allowance of up to 3 million euros, which is de facto a loss cover. The fixed cost grant up to €800 000 and the fixed cost grant phase two with up to €3 million will then be available as an alternative and offer better support than is the case in most other European countries, ladies and gentlemen. Despite a certain degree of hope on the horizon, despite the vaccination, which hopefully will soon also be applicable in Austria, we are not at the end, but In the middle of this global economic and health crisis. It is therefore necessary to extend many of the existing measures and support until this crisis is over. We therefore have a legal overwintering package in order to implement the necessary legal changes, which is available to you under the COVID-19 Tax Act. The application includes the extension of the reduced turnover tax in the areas of gastronomy, hotel industry, culture by the end of 2021. It includes the extension of the tax advantage for workers in different areas, such as the flat rate and other allowances. Also included is the extension of the upcoming tax receipts and the withdrawal of pre-orders of interest rates up to 31. March. All in all, the tax receipts and the reduction of advance payments amounted to EUR 6.5 billion. This was important because liquidity remained directly in the companies. The government fractions will also make a request in the course of the debate to enable an adjusted repayment. So far, the statutory rate payment structure has provided for a maximum of 12 months to be set at an interest rate of 4.5 percent above the basic interest rate in order to repay the tax debt. Now we change this and extend the repayment period to up to 36 months and reduce the interest rate to 2 percent above the basic interest rate. The amendment also includes the implementation of tax-free Christmas vouchers up to 365 euros per employee. You know, the Christmas celebrations can't take place this year, but in order to make it possible for the employees' employees to receive the recognition they deserve, we want to take this measure. Another legislative initiative provides for the innovation of the SME Funding Act and the Guarantee Act of 1977. These changes can extend the liability framework for the so important Covid-19 bridging guarantees by the AWS and the ÖHT beyond the year until the end of June 2021. [[Congratulations on ÖVP and Greens.]] Thank you. Finally, I would like to refer briefly to the COVID-19 Transparency Act, which will also be adopted today. The Ministry of Finance has already partially exceeded the legal requirements for reporting on the Covid-19 assistance measures. Nevertheless, the government has tried to further increase transparency, and we have also been guided by the proposals of Parliament's budget service. Therefore, in the future, in addition to the already existing reporting obligations of the BMF on disbursements from the Covid-19 crisis management fund to the Budget Committee, there will also be a reporting obligation of the individual departments within the respective specialist committees. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e20138	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Especially the entire tourism, the travel industry, the organizers, the leisure industry and also the gastronomy are undoubtedly the main sectors affected by this worldwide corona pandemic. The travel agencies are particularly hard hit, because they naturally live on the freedom of travel, and I really have to contradict Mr Matznetter: From the beginning of this crisis, we have tried to find special solutions for travel agencies. This is an area that is very special. We have already provided the possibility in the fixed cost grant phase that personnel costs could also be claimed, because the entire travel business had to be cancelled from half a year in particular. The fixed cost grant phase one has thus already been specifically adapted to travel agencies, and in particular the fixed cost grant phase two with the loss replacement and the possibility of depreciation of the frustrated expenses will provide massive support for travel agencies, which is correct and good because this is our specialists in Austria for the travel business. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] However, we also see a difficult problem facing us, and that is the question of the insolvency protection of the travel industry. So far, there has been a major global travel insurer, the HDI Global insurance company, which is on 1. Retired from the global insurance market on December 20, 2020. This also affects very many companies in Austria, and all the other insurers have discontinued their offer for package travel insurance in Austria. This is primarily due to the current global pandemic. Since the insurance and banking market is currently not ready to take over this risk for the industry, we have built a temporary system to support the tour operators, the travel agencies as much as possible. This means that we create the basis for an insolvency security through the Austrian Hotel and Tourism Bank for a temporary bridge. The travel agency industry in Austria is characterized by many small companies. It dominates the enterprises with one to nine employees, and in total more than 2 600 companies currently operate the travel agency in Austria. Without this insolvency protection for customer funds – and I believe that is especially important – the Travel operators no longer offer vacation trips, because the EU-Palestal Travel Directive provides for full insolvency protection. This would be like a professional ban. With today's proposal, and I should like to thank the House very much, we are very much in favour of the support that this sector needs for 2021. We will definitely get our travel freedom back next year, and there will need specialists who will ensure holiday experiences. That is why this support in the form of insolvency protection, which we decide today, is so important. Also a very important support measure for the whole tourism, gastronomy and leisure sector is the extension of the tax reduction, the liability package and, above all, the tax relief arrangements. Due to the extension of the sales tax reduction of 5 percent to the entire year 2021, many of the losses are covered. The reduction concerns all foods and drinks, both non-alcoholic and alcoholic. These include shelters and cabins. Also the commercial accommodation, pensions, private room rentals and the transfer of apartments and plots for camping purposes are covered by this extension. In addition, we will continue to make theatre performances, zoological parks, museums, zoological and botanical gardens, nature parks and cinemas benefit from this tax reduction, and also actors, who belong to most of Austria for leisure activities, will benefit from the reduced tax rate. But especially for us, the extension of the liability framework for bridging financing is also crucial. This was actually the first instrument that we launched together with the Austrian Hotel and Tourism Bank – already in March – which Zig thousands of businesses have taken advantage of and with which we have really created the opportunity to bring the companies through this very, very difficult time. This is a measure that is very much accepted. For the ÖHT, a liability framework of EUR 1.6 billion is available, resulting in the extension of the authorisation until 30. ==References== Furthermore, we will continue to extend tax subsidies until 31. ==References== In addition, no interest is required, so this is a very, very crucial and important measure so that we can get our businesses through this time, through this corona crisis. Dear ladies and gentlemen, the gastronomy, the country guest houses, the locals, the bars are a very important part of our social life. The hotel industry, the accommodation industry, the entire tourism industry create unforgettable holiday experiences in Austria. We must also remember our organizers who contribute to the fact that the entire economic area is alive when you think of fairs, congresses, and it is above all our leisure activities that always offer us recreation and entertainment. We are a country of cuisine, we are a country of hospitality, and we must do everything we can to bring these industries through this particularly difficult and hard time. I should like to ask you, ladies and gentlemen, for support. – Thank you very much.

That's over a million people; 1.2 million Austrians already use it, not only once a year, but daily or often, for different services. We also need to give you the opportunity to use this service accordingly. To address some of your topics: The Data Protection Council has been mentioned. We were at the meeting of the Data Protection Council. I can assure you that the Data Protection Council has delivered opinions, we have dealt with them point by point, and these proposals from different areas have been incorporated into numerous amendments to the assessment text. I would like to thank all those who have participated in the video date I have offered, all from the FID Committee and others, where we have been able to discuss many of the open questions accordingly. Estonia has been addressed. – Yes, Estonia has built its solutions, its digital management on the green meadow. We are a country that has a long tradition in administration, similar to Germany and other countries. If you look at Malta, for example, this is also a very small state, you can see that it is important that we also allow direct access to those who are not so much at home in this digital world that the official routes can all be made personally. This is very important to me, we have a large share of older people. Even if, in a certain situation of life, we decide that we like to go personally, then it must and must be possible. This is not possible, for example, in countries that are always mentioned, but which are behind us as far as digital administration, such as Denmark, is concerned. I can assure you that we will not, for us it is always important that the option exists. It has also been addressed the topic of the digital divide. I would also like to address and invite you to this, because we too have such a platform, not only Finland. We have the platform Fit4Internet, where courses are offered for older people, which we have forced. We have even changed to television now, because it is no longer possible for the older generation to be reached in person. To close the digital divide is, I believe, something that unites us and where there are appropriate measures. The security of electronic identity and electronic identification shall be ensured, as it shall be completely different: Nothing is stored in this electronic identity, there are no data on it. The security measures such as the Irisscan or the fingerprint remain on the device, which are not transferred anywhere. What you can do with it is to take office – and that is safer than it was before, because this security is also due to the fact that this electronic identity is issuing an authority for the first time. In that case, it's the passport authority, that's this first check. If you have this electronic identity then you can use it for office routes, but you don't have to. It is not the comprehensive step in a digital administration, but it is a very important point that we pick up here, that we fulfil this point, that we do not go back there and offer additional services to Austrians, so I ask you very much for your support.

It has also been mentioned that the higher investment premium of 14 percent is mainly intended for investments in forward-looking projects – ecologisation and digitalisation. The Federal Minister has already carried it out: We can see from the measures that they really work very well. The lever is there, which in about EUR 2.5 billion, which has so far been promised to invest in investment premiums, trigger over EUR 20 billion. I think that's a program that can be seen more than that.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e24941	Mr President. Estimated Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Dear spectators at home! I should also like to speak about the two government documents. For the first time, the Federal Act, which amends the Electricity Economic and Organisation Act, in short ElWOG. It really is about the most important law of today. It is about the Austrian network reserve, our blackout insurance for Austria. We want to ensure that our domestic power grid is not overshadowed by the transport of energy through our lines. The Austrian electricity grid is also part of the European electricity grid and its markets. Through the cross-border electricity transit, which is in itself necessary, is becoming increasingly common in the Austrian electricity grid. In order to avoid these bottlenecks, on the one hand, we need bottleneck management and on the other hand large reserves. It is therefore also necessary to lay down in this law the rules for the supply and supply of electricity. Firstly, we want to regulate how many reserves we need, second, as we do when power generation plants are shut down, thirdly, we create clear rules so that a power generation plant cannot simply get off the grid, and there can also be small installations for the first time in the application of grid reserves. Furthermore, we also comply with a European directive: making calls for tenders more transparent and competitive. This is a good legal situation compared to the current regulation. Our Blackout insurance for our network stability and security of supply is thus also completed. I can also thank all the energy speakers for the talks, and, Mr Kassinger, I also agree with you that we can and should speak about the way in which processes are handled. Here I am quite with you that we have opportunities to improve when we sit at a table together. Secondly, to the Federal Act, which amends the Ökostromgesetz 2012 and the KWK Act. The colleague has already mentioned this in detail. It is a question of extending the time limits for the start-up of installations. The Ecostrom Act proposes fixed deadlines, and the problem has arisen that in certain installations due to Corona it is not possible to comply with the deadlines because of the lack of components, and therefore we want to extend the deadlines. In concrete terms, we extend the time limit for the start-up of eco-electric power plants to be funded by means of feed-in tariffs or investment subsidies by 12 months, and for the CHP installations which are supported by investment aid, the period will be extended by six months. In addition, the regulation of support applications for photovoltaic systems, which is also very important and expires in 2020, will be extended by one year. I am also seeking your approval on behalf of the applicants who make a significant contribution to the future of renewable energy, so that they do not lose their contribution indebtedly. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e25192	The amendment has been properly tabled and explained in its main guidelines. Mr Lukas Hammer is speaking on behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2020-12-10-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00069_d8e25219	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Even if it's a little late: I also believe that this is one of the more important laws we decide today. Today, if we decide by a majority – but it looks good – is an important and a good day for security of supply in Austria. Today, we are adopting an EU-wide network reserve. Why is that important? – It has already been mentioned: Simply put, it's about our power grids not going out of the air. Network bottlenecks arise – you know that – when a lot of electricity is passed through Austria – we are, so to speak, a transit country for electricity – or if electricity production cannot cover consumption. As our networks are very heavily burdened, there needs to be bottleneck management, and with this network reserve we ensure that. We now require the leader of the control zone, that is, the APG, to have a transparent analysis of how much spare capacity will be available next year. On the basis of this analysis, the capacity must be tendered out in a transparent process and sufficient capacities then be countered. It is now new that industrial plants – and I think this is particularly important – can also participate in this network reserve. In other words, it is not only about providing additional power plant capacities in the short term, but also about reducing consumption in the short term. I would like to address the situation in which we were: The current network reserve scheme has never been notified to the EU Commission. As early as 2018, the EU Commission has in principle made it clear that this is an unauthorised aid. That means we really needed action. In terms of content, for example, there was no transparent tender and there was only a limited bidder circle. Since then, in other words since 2018, there have been discussions with the European Commission. These discussions continued even now, after the government template was presented. That's why we got a little stress and time scarcity, so to speak. There has been a need for change in order to ensure that this is truly EU-wide, because imagine this: If we now decide on a regulation that concludes the APG contracts for the network reserve and then the EU Commission tells us that the EU is illegal, then we must re-interpret these contracts! That would be a disaster. That is why I am very glad that we have now found a good solution in this narrow corset, which the European Commission tells us to do. I therefore make the following request, which, as far as I know, has been distributed to you: [[Quoted print version removed]] I would like to explain the amendment in the main guidelines: The main focus is the redesign of the auction design, which is now limited to the tendering of three different products – a two-year product, a one-year product and a seasonal product – and that is what I think is important, because this is also a cost question and also the question of who can participate – the cheapest combination of the received offers. There are also adjustments to the system analysis, in which transparency and additions can be taken into account as regards the costs incurred in the provision of the network reserve. I believe that this is a very good, cost-effective solution for the network reserve, which really guarantees that our electricity networks will continue to be stable in the future and that they will meet the challenges that the energy transition entails. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I would like to conclude with a thank you: first of all, thanks to the staff of the Ministry of Climate Change, who have worked tirelessly over the last two years, but still increased and condensed in the last week, also on the weekend and on the holiday, to the fact that we now have a good and sustainable solution. This is already based on the 1979 Geneva Convention, and it is very specific about health protection, which is important in relation to highly toxic, highly toxic air pollutants. This Geneva Convention, with its eight follow-up protocols, is part of the subject we are deciding on today. Following this, there is still the Stockholm Protocol of 2001, in which at that time the twelve most toxic and harmful persistent organic pollutants were listed and banned. Why do you need such a thing? – What is hidden behind the abbreviation POP is in fact a very serious and important issue. It is highly harmful, long-lasting, long-lasting pollutants that are resistant to degradation processes and can therefore be detected in the environment for a very long time. They are very volatile and can be tolerated in gas and dust particles over long distances, that is, you can find them practically already on earth. They are not biodegradable, they are not water soluble and therefore pass through the tissue in animals and humans. They are proven in human and animal breast milk and harmful to human health. They are carcinogenic, they lead to immune defects, they accumulate in the body of animals and humans. Therefore, international agreements are urgently needed to eliminate precisely these harmful substances, eliminate and stop products and their use. There are products like DDT among them, which is certainly the most famous and many from earlier times a term. Today it is PCBs, these are polychlorinated biphenyls, which are still present in capacitors, hydraulic fluids, plasticizers of surfaces, seals and varnishes and are therefore still present. In the event of the demolition of buildings, where defects occur in such plants, there is the highest risk, at the highest risk. There are safety rules that allow the use of PCBs in inhabited areas, including schools and hospitals, to take all measures to prevent electrotechnical disturbances that may lead to fire. Installations shall be checked regularly for leaks. This is therefore a serious and important issue, so I would like to ask the Members of the Freedom Party once again to agree to this request, because it is a matter of urgency. to the very important health protection. Thank you.

As part of the Stockholm Convention, a list of the most dangerous POP substances was drawn up for the first time before the turn of the millennium and a ban on these substances was imposed in those countries which ratified this State Treaty. The biofuels report says that we save around 1.5 million tonnes of CO2 by attaching biogenic fuels to fossil fuels. So we produce E10 or B10 and we use pollutants for it! It is not about sprinkling high-quality food, but we often have corn that is brushed, which cannot even be used as a feed, and this can be used for it, or old fats, old food oils. Austria is a technology leader. Let us take advantage of this, this is my appeal to all the parliamentary parties. - Thank you.
